# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

## User Profile

Joshua works on LSATU teaching, student records, curriculum quality, releases, and a Windows/AI-tool environment. He uses Codex for evidence-based implementation, independent review, connected-app drafting, recovery, and live-system checks. He values concept-first teaching, plain language for nontechnical workflows, and a hard distinction between source/static evidence and deployment or signed-in acceptance.

## User preferences

- Preserve strict scope: honor "Do not commit, push or publish," keep edits in the named path, and report deviations or blocked checks explicitly.
- Validate behavior, not just prose: make predictions, run broken and fixed cases, retain failure evidence/receipts, and distinguish direct proof from inference.
- Do not fabricate browser, device, deployment, persistence, permission, or acceptance evidence; keep tickets open until the requested paths are actually evidenced.
- For student lessons, teach the exact recorded sticking point first; use visual ordinary-language explanation before notation and preserve modality, complete conditions, and fact-versus-rule distinctions.
- When reusing student work, locate and assign the exact existing artifact rather than recreating or paraphrasing it; use a dry run and read-after-write verification for live assignments.
- Use the Codex in-app browser for LSAT Lab checks. Respect explicit approval gates; pilot approval does not authorize a later load.
- For shared Windows tools, diagnose before reinstalling and document availability for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity. Use separate PowerShell commands when requested: "ONE COMMAND PER CALL."
- For connected-app drafting, "Show the draft here; do not send, post, or modify anything." Match real demonstrated style.
- For research synthesis, retain only claims corroborated across two independent sources, each with an exact quote and source.

## General Tips

- Separate focused test/typecheck/build, deployment, and fresh signed-in acceptance; static approval is not visual, keyboard, interaction, or publication proof.
- Resume work by reconciling handoffs with current diffs, live configuration/database pulls, and `wiki/hot.md`; stale checkouts and status claims are recurring hazards.
- Database content writes: snapshot first, reconcile overlapping proposals, guard exact old text/MD5, use approved direct transport, then server-side audit. Do not hand-type huge SQL payloads.
- Windows `command not found` can be stale inherited PATH: check executable/WinGet/saved PATH, refresh or restart the app before reinstalling.
- Preserve unrelated edits, local conflicts, recovery data, secrets, and active browser/session state. GitHub parity is not laptop parity.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. # Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 11.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"305c8f","wall_time_seconds":0.3043353,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1330,"output":"# Step 3 brief: rewrite weak LR explanations in LSAT U\\n\\nYou rewrite explanations that students read after answering an LSAT Logical Reasoning question. You write NOTHING to any database. You produce a proposal file; an independent verifier checks every item before anything is applied. Precision beats volume.\\n\\n## Your inputs\\nBatch file: a JSON list of qids. Per qid, a packet at `PACKETS/<qid>.json` (load with `python -I`, never read big files wholesale). A packet has: the question (stimulus, stem, five choices, key; `bank` is what students actually read, so quote the bank wording when it differs from the DB), the current explanation (`steps` with position/label/body/row_id; `notes` with letter/body/row_id), and `known_problem` (a reader\'s note on what is wrong; it may be stale or wrong).\\n\\nRead the standard before writing:\\n- `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/corpus/lr-explanations/golden-set.md` (what top-tier looks like) and `golden-set-observations.md` (step structure per question type, rubric).\\n- `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/wiki/teaching-voice.md`, `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md` (voice), `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`.\\n- Study 2 to 3 strong explanations of the same type: `python -I C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/system/scripts/lr-explanations/show.py PT146-S1-Q21` prints one in full (use golden-set qids of your question\'s family). Model the structure, never copy sentences or examples. Two golden picks (PT145-S2-Q23, PT138-S2-Q25) are reference-only because their logic is wrong; do not use them.\\n\\n## For each qid\\n1. Read the stimulus, stem, all five choices and the key from the packet. Work out the correct answer yourself first. If you disagree with the key, do not rewrite: output a `flag`.\\n2. Read the current explanation. Decide:\\n   - `rewrite`: it is weak, thin, wrongly registered, wrong, or missing parts.\\n   - `skip`: it is already good (record why in one sentence). Do not churn good text.\\n3. A rewrite keeps the existing step structure and reuses each existing row. For every existing step keep its `row_id`, and set `body` (and `label` if needed). Keep a step\'s body verbatim when it is already sound; improve only what is weak. Do not drop any existing step; if one is junk, rewrite it into something useful. You may add steps (row_id null). If the explanation has no breakdown steps, create the standard set for the type (usually Conclusion, Evidence, Evaluate, Goal; see golden-set-observations for other types).\\n4. Choice notes: exactly five, letters A to E, each reusing its `row_id` (null if the letter has no row). Standards:\\n   - Correct-answer note: teaches the move so a student can repeat it on the next question (what in the choice matches the prediction, what it does with the stimulus\'s words).\\n   - Each wrong-answer note: says exactly why it fails, tied to the choice\'s own words and the stimulus, and usually gives a test or counterexample. 1 to 3 sentences, about 20 to 60 words. The most tempting trap may run longer.\\n   - Never a bare verdict (\\"This is irrelevant\\"). Never a role label as the whole note. Never restate the choice and stop.\\n5. Re-read every quote against the choice or stimulus: exact match to the bank wording. Check every logical claim, especially conditionals (sufficient vs necessary, contrapositive, negation).\\n\\n## Writing rules (hard)\\n- No em dashes or en dashes used as dashes. Use a comma, colon, period, or a new sentence. Arrows `→` are fine in diagrams (one chain per line).\\n- No gendered pronouns (he, she, his, her, him, hers, himself, herself) for people in the stimulus. Use the name, \\"the author\\", \\"the speaker\\", or \\"they\\".\\n- Never mention Logic Games. Never claim answer-position patterns. No jokes about students, no politics, no \\"this is always wrong\\" overbroad rules.\\n- No internal labels in student text: no \\"concept lesson\\", \\"golden set\\", \\"Revamp\\", production metadata. No filler (\\"Great question\\", \\"it\'s important to note\\", \\"that\'s the point\\"). No word \\"door\\" as a unit of content.\\n- Plain, direct, confident, short sentences. Teach the reasoning; do not assert the conclusion. Concrete beats abstract. Speak to the student as \\"we\\" or \\"you\\". A personal jab at the test is fine, sparingly.\\n- Keep paragraph breaks as `\\\\n\\\\n` inside a body when helpful. Do not use markdown headers or bullets unless the existing steps already do.\\n- Never change which choice is credited.\\n\\n## Output\\nWrite JSON Lines to the output path you are given, one object per qid, in input order:\\n`{\\"qid\\": \\"...\\", \\"action\\": \\"rewrite\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"<from packet>\\", \\"steps\\": [{\\"row_id\\": \\"<uuid or null>\\", \\"position\\": 1, \\"label\\": \\"...\\", \\"body\\": \\"...\\"}], \\"notes\\": [{\\"row_id\\": \\"<uuid or null>\\", \\"letter\\": \\"A\\", \\"body\\": \\"...\\"}], \\"why\\": \\"one sentence: what was weak\\", \\"neighbors\\": [\\"qids you modelled on\\"]}`\\nor `{\\"qid\\": \\"...\\", \\"action\\": \\"skip\\", \\"why\\": \\"...\\"}` or `{\\"qid\\": \\"...\\", \\"action\\": \\"flag\\", \\"why\\": \\"suspected mis-key or bank/DB mismatch: ...\\"}`.\\nIn a rewrite, `steps` lists ALL steps (existing in position order, then any added) and `notes` lists all five.\\n\\nWrite the file with a Python script that builds dicts and `json.dumps(..., ensure_ascii=False)`; put scripts only in your work directory. Double-check each item against the question before you finish.\\nReply with: counts of rewrite, skip, flag; and any item you are unsure of, with the reason.\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b9f595","wall_time_seconds":0.2706783,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":618,"output":"# Step 3 verification: independent check of rewritten LR explanations\\n\\nAnother worker rewrote LSAT U Logical Reasoning explanations. You are the independent check. Nothing is applied unless you approve it. Be skeptical: catch errors, do not admire the prose.\\n\\n## Inputs\\n- Proposals: JSONL at the path you are given (see BRIEF.md in this folder for the format and the writing rules the writer had to follow).\\n- Packets with the question and the OLD explanation: `PACKETS/<qid>.json` (bank = what students read; DB = admin copy; quote-check against the bank wording).\\nLoad JSON with `python -I`. Scripts go only in your work directory.\\n\\n## For every `rewrite` item\\nSolve the question yourself first from the stimulus and choices. Then check the full new explanation, step by step and note by note:\\n1. Logic: every claim about the argument, conditionals, quantifiers, causation and each choice is correct. The correct letter is argued as correct and every other letter as wrong, for the right reason. Prediction in the Goal step matches the credited answer.\\n2. Quotes: every quoted phrase matches the bank wording exactly.\\n3. Teaching: wrong-answer notes give a real reason (not a verdict), the correct note teaches a repeatable move, steps are not padded, nothing good in the old text was destroyed without reason.\\n4. Rules: no em or en dashes as dashes; no gendered pronouns for stimulus people; no Logic Games; no overbroad \\"always wrong\\" rules; no internal labels or filler; reads like plain direct teaching.\\n5. Structure: every old step row_id is present and none dropped; exactly five notes A to E; reused row_ids belong to the right letter/position.\\n\\nVerdicts: `approve`, `fix` (give the full corrected item in `new`: the complete steps and notes lists in the proposal\'s format; fix only what is wrong), or `reject` (say why; wrong logic you cannot fix with confidence, key disagreement, etc.).\\nFor `skip` and `flag` items: agree or not. If you disagree with a skip (the old text is in fact weak or wrong), verdict `reject_skip` with the reason. If a flag claims a mis-key, solve the question and say whether the key is right.\\n\\n## Output\\nJSON Lines at the output path, one per proposal in input order:\\n`{\\"qid\\": \\"...\\", \\"verdict\\": \\"approve|fix|reject|agree_skip|reject_skip|agree_flag|reject_flag\\", \\"new\\": {\\"steps\\": [...], \\"notes\\": [...]} (only for fix), \\"note\\": \\"one sentence\\"}`\\nReply with counts per verdict and list every fix/reject with its one-sentence reason.\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"0b9414","wall_time_seconds":0.2524543,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1436,"output":"\\"\\"\\"Build guarded SQL for step 3.\\r\\nUsage: python -I apply3.py <out_prefix> <proposals.jsonl> <verdicts.jsonl> [more pairs...]\\r\\nResolves each rewrite to its final steps/notes (proposal, or verifier\'s fix), runs hard gates,\\r\\nthen writes guarded UPDATEs (old body must still match) and guarded INSERTs, plus <prefix>-final.json (old kept as snapshot).\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nimport json, sys, re, os, hashlib\\r\\nmd5 = lambda s: hashlib.md5(s.encode(\'utf-8\')).hexdigest()\\r\\nsys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nHERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\\r\\nGENDER = re.compile(r\\"\\\\b(he|she|him|her|his|hers|himself|herself)\\\\b\\", re.I)\\r\\nprefix = sys.argv[1]\\r\\npairs = [(sys.argv[i], sys.argv[i + 1]) for i in range(2, len(sys.argv), 2)]\\r\\nq = lambda s: \\"\'\\" + s.replace(\\"\'\\", \\"\'\'\\") + \\"\'\\"\\r\\nfinal, held = [], []\\r\\nfor pf, vf in pairs:\\r\\n    props = [json.loads(l) for l in open(pf, encoding=\'utf-8\') if l.strip()]\\r\\n    verd = {}\\r\\n    for l in open(vf, encoding=\'utf-8\'):\\r\\n        if l.strip():\\r\\n            v = json.loads(l); verd[v[\'qid\']] = v\\r\\n    for p in props:\\r\\n        if p.get(\'action\') != \'rewrite\': continue\\r\\n        v = verd.get(p[\'qid\'])\\r\\n        if not v: held.append((p[\'qid\'], \'no verdict\')); continue\\r\\n        if v[\'verdict\'] not in (\'approve\', \'fix\'): held.append((p[\'qid\'], v[\'verdict\'] + \': \' + v.get(\'note\', \'\'))); continue\\r\\n        steps, notes = (v[\'new\'][\'steps\'], v[\'new\'][\'notes\']) if v[\'verdict\'] == \'fix\' else (p[\'steps\'], p[\'notes\'])\\r\\n        pk = json.load(open(f\\"{HERE}/packets/{p[\'qid\']}.json\\", encoding=\'utf-8\'))\\r\\n        old_steps = {s[\'row_id\']: s for s in pk[\'steps\']}\\r\\n        old_notes = {n[\'row_id\']: n for n in pk[\'notes\']}\\r\\n        text = \' \'.join([s[\'body\'] + \' \' + (s[\'label\'] or \'\') for s in steps] + [n[\'body\'] for n in notes])\\r\\n        old_text = \' \'.join([s[\'body\'] + \' \' + (s[\'label\'] or \'\') for s in pk[\'steps\']] + [n[\'body\'] for n in pk[\'notes\']])\\r\\n        err = None\\r\\n        if \'—\' in text or \'–\' in text: err = \'dash\'\\r\\n        elif len(GENDER.findall(text)) > len(GENDER.findall(old_text)): err = \'pronoun\'\\r\\n        elif \'logic game\' in text.lower(): err = \'logic games\'\\r\\n        elif sorted(n[\'letter\'] for n in notes) != list(\'ABCDE\'): err = \'notes not A-E\'\\r\\n        elif any(not n[\'body\'].strip() for n in notes) or any(not s[\'body\'].strip() for s in steps): err = \'empty body\'\\r\\n        elif set(old_steps) - {s[\'row_id\'] for s in steps}: err = \'dropped a step row\'\\r\\n        elif any(s[\'row_id\'] and s[\'row_id\'] not in old_steps for s in steps) or any(n[\'row_id\'] and n[\'row_id\'] not in old_notes for n in notes): err = \'unknown row id\'\\r\\n        if err: held.append((p[\'qid\'], \'gate: \' + err)); continue\\r\\n        final.append({\'qid\': p[\'qid\'], \'explanation_id\': pk[\'explanation_id\'], \'steps\': steps, \'notes\': notes, \'old_steps\': pk[\'steps\'], \'old_notes\': pk[\'notes\']})\\r\\nprint(\'final\', len(final), \'held\', len(held))\\r\\nfor h in held: print(\'  HELD\', h)\\r\\njson.dump(final, open(prefix + \'-final.json\', \'w\', encoding=\'utf-8\'), ensure_ascii=False)\\r\\nupd_steps, upd_notes, ins_steps, ins_notes = [], [], [], []\\r\\nfor f in final:\\r\\n    os_ = {s[\'row_id\']: s for s in f[\'old_steps\']}; on = {n[\'row_id\']: n for n in f[\'old_notes\']}\\r\\n    for s in f[\'steps\']:\\r\\n        if s[\'row_id\']:\\r\\n            o = os_[s[\'row_id\']]\\r\\n            if o[\'body\'] != s[\'body\'] or (o[\'label\'] or \'\') != (s[\'label\'] or \'\'): upd_steps.append((s[\'row_id\'], o[\'body\'], s[\'body\'], o[\'label\'] or \'\', s[\'label\'] or \'\'))\\r\\n        else: ins_steps.append((f[\'explanation_id\'], s[\'position\'], s[\'label\'] or \'\', s[\'body\']))\\r\\n    for n in f[\'notes\']:\\r\\n        if n[\'row_id\']:\\r\\n            o = on[n[\'row_id\']]\\r\\n            if o[\'body\'] != n[\'body\']: upd_notes.append((n[\'row_id\'], o[\'body\'], n[\'body\']))\\r\\n        else: ins_notes.append((f[\'explanation_id\'], n[\'letter\'], n[\'body\']))\\r\\nprint(\'updates: steps\', len(upd_steps), \'notes\', len(upd_notes), \'| inserts: steps\', len(ins_steps), \'notes\', len(ins_notes))\\r\\nfiles = []\\r\\ndef chunks(rows, size_of):\\r\\n    cur, sz = [], 0\\r\\n    for r in rows:\\r\\n        s = size_of(r)\\r\\n        if cur and sz + s > 50000: yield cur; cur, sz = [], 0\\r\\n        cur.append(r); sz += s\\r\\n    if cur: yield cur\\r\\nfor rows in chunks(upd_steps, lambda r: len(r[2]) + 200):\\r\\n    vals = \',\\\\n\'.join(f\\"({q(r[0])}::uuid,{q(md5(r[1]))},{q(r[2])},{q(r[3])},{q(r[4])})\\" for r in rows)\\r\\n    files.append(f\\"update lr_explanation_steps t set body=v.nb, label=case when v.nl=\'\' then t.label else v.nl end from (values\\\\n{vals}\\\\n) v(id,ob,nb,ol,nl) where t.id=v.id and md5(t.body)=v.ob returning t.id;\\")\\r\\nfor rows in chunks(upd_notes, lambda r: len(r[2]) + 100):\\r\\n    vals = \',\\\\n\'.join(f\\"({q(r[0])}::uuid,{q(md5(r[1]))},{q(r[2])})\\" for r in rows)\\r\\n    files.append(f\\"update lr_explanation_choice_notes t set body=v.nb from (values\\\\n{vals}\\\\n) v(id,ob,nb) where t.id=v.id and md5(t.body)=v.ob returning t.id;\\")\\r\\nfor rows in chunks(ins_steps, lambda r: len(r[3]) + 200):\\r\\n    vals = \',\\\\n\'.join(f\\"({q(r[0])}::uuid,{r[1]},{q(r[2])},{q(r[3])})\\" for r in rows)\\r\\n    files.append(f\\"insert into lr_explanation_steps (explanation_id,position,label,body) select v.e,v.p,v.l,v.b from (values\\\\n{vals}\\\\n) v(e,p,l,b) where not exists (select 1 from lr_explanation_steps x where x.explanation_id=v.e and x.position=v.p) returning id;\\")\\r\\nfor rows in chunks(ins_notes, lambda r: len(r[2]) + 150):\\r\\n    vals = \',\\\\n\'.join(f\\"({q(r[0])}::uuid,{q(r[1])},{q(r[2])})\\" for r in rows)\\r\\n    files.append(f\\"insert into lr_explanation_choice_notes (explanation_id,letter,body) select v.e,v.l,v.b from (values\\\\n{vals}\\\\n) v(e,l,b) where not exists (select 1 from lr_explanation_choice_notes x where x.explanation_id=v.e and x.letter=v.l) returning id;\\")\\r\\nfor i, s in enumerate(files, 1):\\r\\n    open(f\\"{prefix}-{i:02d}.sql\\", \'w\', encoding=\'utf-8\').write(s)\\r\\nprint(len(files), \'sql files\')\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

expected=v[\\\"new\\\"] if v[\\\"verdict\\\"]==\\\"fix\\\" else pr\\n  for key in [\\\"steps\\\",\\\"notes\\\"]:\\n   if expected[key]!=f[key]: issues.append((f[\\\"qid\\\"],key,\\\"final mismatch\\\"))\\n  pk=json.loads((p/f\\\"packets/{f[chr(113)+chr(105)+chr(100)]}.json\\\").read_text(encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\"))\\n  if pk[\\\"steps\\\"]!=f[\\\"old_steps\\\"] or pk[\\\"notes\\\"]!=f[\\\"old_notes\\\"]: issues.append((f[\\\"qid\\\"],\\\"snapshot mismatch\\\"))\\n  for key,field in [(\\\"steps\\\",\\\"position\\\"),(\\\"notes\\\",\\\"letter\\\")]:\\n   old={x[\\\"row_id\\\"]:x for x in pk[key]}\\n   for x in f[key]:\\n    if x[\\\"row_id\\\"] and old[x[\\\"row_id\\\"]][field]!=x[field]: issues.append((f[\\\"qid\\\"],key,\\\"mapping mismatch\\\"))\\n   ids=[x[\\\"row_id\\\"] for x in f[key] if x[\\\"row_id\\\"]]\\n   if len(ids)!=len(set(ids)): issues.append((f[\\\"qid\\\"],key,\\\"duplicate id\\\"))\\n  positions=[s[\\\"position\\\"] for s in f[\\\"steps\\\"]]\\n  if len(positions)!=len(set(positions)): issues.append((f[\\\"qid\\\"],\\\"duplicate position\\\"))\\n  counts[\\\"step_updates\\\"]+=sum(1 for s in f[\\\"steps\\\"] if s[\\\"row_id\\\"] and any(o[\\\"row_id\\\"]==s[\\\"row_id\\\"] and (o[\\\"body\\\"]!=s[\\\"body\\\"] or (o[\\\"label\\\"] or \\\"\\\")!=(s[\\\"label\\\"] or \\\"\\\")) for o in f[\\\"old_steps\\\"]))\\n  counts[\\\"note_updates\\\"]+=sum(1 for n in f[\\\"notes\\\"] if n[\\\"row_id\\\"] and any(o[\\\"row_id\\\"]==n[\\\"row_id\\\"] and o[\\\"body\\\"]!=n[\\\"body\\\"] for o in f[\\\"old_notes\\\"]))\\n  counts[\\\"step_inserts\\\"]+=sum(s[\\\"row_id\\\"] is None for s in f[\\\"steps\\\"])\\n  counts[\\\"note_inserts\\\"]+=sum(n[\\\"row_id\\\"] is None for n in f[\\\"notes\\\"])\\n'); text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nimport json,pathlib,collections\np=pathlib.Path('.')\nissues=[]; counts=collections.Counter()\nload=lambda f: json.loads(f.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))\nlines=lambda f: [json.loads(l) for l in f.read_text(encoding='utf-8').splitlines() if l.strip()]\nqids=sum([load(p/f'batch-{i:02}.json') for i in range(1,25)],[])\nprint('batch total',len(qids),'unique',len(set(qids)))\nfor i in range(22,25):\n props={x['qid']:x for x in lines(p/f'out-{i:02}.jsonl')}\n verd={x['qid']:x for x in lines(p/f'verd-{i:02}.jsonl')}\n finals=load(p/f'sql/e{i:02}-final.json')\n print(i,'finals',len(finals))\n for f in finals:\n  v=verd[f['qid']]; expected=v['new'] if v['verdict']=='fix' else props[f['qid']]\n  for key in ['steps','notes']:\n   if expected[key]!=f[key]: issues.append((f['qid'],key,'final mismatch'))\n  pk=load(p/f\"packets/{f['qid']}.json\")\n  if pk['steps']!=f['old_steps'] or pk['notes']!=f['old_notes']: issues.append((f['qid'],'snapshot mismatch'))\n  for key,field in [('steps','position'),('notes','letter')]:\n   old={x['row_id']:x for x in pk[key]}\n   for x in f[key]:\n    if x['row_id'] and old[x['row_id']][field]!=x[field]: issues.append((f['qid'],key,'mapping mismatch'))\n   ids=[x['row_id'] for x in f[key] if x['row_id']]\n   if len(ids)!=len(set(ids)): issues.append((f['qid'],key,'duplicate id'))\n  positions=[s['position'] for s in f['steps']]\n  if len(positions)!=len(set(positions)): issues.append((f['qid'],'duplicate position'))\n  counts['step_updates']+=sum(1 for s in f['steps'] if s['row_id'] and any(o['row_id']==s['row_id'] and (o['body']!=s['body'] or (o['label'] or '')!=(s['label'] or '')) for o in f['old_steps']))\n  counts['note_updates']+=sum(1 for n in f['notes'] if n['row_id'] and any(o['row_id']==n['row_id'] and o['body']!=n['body'] for o in f['old_notes']))\n  counts['step_inserts']+=sum(s['row_id'] is None for s in f['steps'])\n  counts['note_inserts']+=sum(n['row_id'] is None for n in f['notes'])\nprint('ISSUES',issues,'COUNTS',dict(counts))\n'@ | python -I -","workdir":"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\699dc587-39bd-4a08-a0c2-306235d0ae46\\scratchpad\\step3","max_output_tokens":2000}));

## assistant

Artifacts for batches 22–24 are complete: 18 rewrites, 13 approvals, 5 verifier corrections. All final JSON matches the approved proposal or corrected verifier item. Existing row IDs, step positions, note letters, and snapshots match the packets; no duplicate IDs or positions found.

These batches expect 41 step updates, 90 note updates, and 2 new steps.

Created read-only audit queries and `audit-codex/manifest.json` for all canonical finals. They cover 191 unique questions and 1,687 expected rows, including 75 additions. All 1,612 existing row IDs are unique. Batch 07’s `b07`, `c07`, and `d07` final files are byte-identical.

The original SQL generator has incomplete gates: it does not validate note letter/row mapping or step positions, permits unchanged gendered-pronoun counts, and guards updates by body MD5 without guarding labels or ownership. The new audit queries independently check those row identities, slots, labels, duplicates, final hashes, and old hashes.

No live queries or content writes performed.

s:hashlib.md5(s.encode()).hexdigest()\nsummary=[]\nfor b in range(22,25):\n fs=json.loads((p/f'sql/e{b}-final.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8')); rows=[]; counts={'updated_steps':0,'updated_notes':0,'inserted_steps':0,'inserted_notes':0}\n for f in fs:\n  for kind,key,slot in [('step','steps','position'),('note','notes','letter')]:\n   old={x['row_id']:x for x in f['old_'+key]}\n   for x in f[key]:\n    o=old.get(x['row_id']); change=not o or o['body']!=x['body'] or kind=='step' and (o['label'] or '')!=(x['label'] or '')\n    if change: counts[('updated_' if o else 'inserted_')+key]+=1\n    rows.append('('+','.join(q(v) for v in [f['qid'],kind,f['explanation_id'],str(x[slot]),x['row_id'],md(o['body']) if o else None,(o['label'] or '') if o and kind=='step' else None,x['body'],(x['label'] or '') if kind=='step' else None])+','+('true' if change else 'false')+')')\n sql='with expected(qid,kind,eid,slot,rid,old_md5,old_label,new_body,new_label,changed) as (values\\n'+',\\n'.join(rows)+'''),\nlocked_steps as materialized (select t.* from lr_explanation_steps t where t.explanation_id::text in (select eid from expected) for update),\nlocked_notes as materialized (select t.* from lr_explanation_choice_notes t where t.explanation_id::text in (select eid from expected) for update),\nactual as (select 'step'::text kind,id::text rid,explanation_id::text eid,position::text slot,md5(body) body_md5,coalesce(label,'') label from locked_steps union all select 'note',id::text,explanation_id::text,letter::text,md5(body),null::text from locked_notes),\neligibility as materialized (select not exists (\n select 1 from expected e where\n (e.rid is not null and (select count(*) from actual a where a.kind=e.kind and a.eid=e.eid and a.slot=e.slot)=1 and not exists(select 1 from actual a where a.kind=e.kind and a.eid=e.eid and a.slot=e.slot and a.rid=e.rid and a.body_md5=e.old_md5 and (e.kind='note' or a.label=e.old_label)))\n or (e.rid is not null and (select count(*) from actual a where a.kind=e.kind and a.eid=e.eid and a.slot=e.slot)<>1)\n or (e.rid is null and exists(select 1 from actual a where a.kind=e.kind and a.eid=e.eid and a.slot=e.slot))\n ) and not exists(select 1 from actual a where not exists(select 1 from expected e where a.kind=e.kind and a.eid=e.eid and a.slot=e.slot)) eligible),\nus as (update lr_explanation_steps t set body=e.new_body,label=e.new_label from expected e,eligibility g where g.eligible and e.kind='step' and e.changed and e.rid is not null and t.id=e.rid::uuid and t.explanation_id=e.eid::uuid and t.position::text=e.slot and md5(t.body)=e.old_md5 and coalesce(t.label,'')=e.old_label returning t.id),\nun as (update lr_explanation_choice_notes t set body=e.new_body from expected e,eligibility g where g.eligible and e.kind='note' and e.changed and e.rid is not null and t.id=e.rid::uuid and t.explanation_id=e.eid::uuid and t.letter::text=e.slot and md5(t.body)=e.old_md5 returning t.id),\nis_ as (insert into lr_explanation_steps(explanation_id,position,label,body) select e.eid::uuid,e.slot::integer,e.new_label,e.new_body from expected e,eligibility g where g.eligible and e.kind='step' and e.rid is null and not exists(select 1 from lr_explanation_steps t where t.explanation_id=e.eid::uuid and t.position::text=e.slot) returning id),\nin_ as (insert into lr_explanation_choice_notes(explanation_id,letter,body) select e.eid::uuid,e.slot,e.new_body from expected e,eligibility g where g.eligible and e.kind='note' and e.rid is null and not exists(select 1 from lr_explanation_choice_notes t where t.explanation_id=e.eid::uuid and t.letter::text=e.slot) returning id),\nresult as (select (select eligible from eligibility) eligible,(select count(*) from us) updated_steps,(select count(*) from un) updated_notes,(select count(*) from is_) inserted_steps,(select count(*) from in_) inserted_notes)\nselect *,1/(case when not eligible or ('''+ ' and '.join(f'{k}={v}' for k,v in counts.items())+''') then 1 else 0 end) atomic_assertion from result;\n'''\n dest=out/f'apply-atomic-e{b}.sql'; dest.write_text(sql,encoding='utf-8'); summary.append({'batch':b,'path':str(dest.resolve()),'expected_writes':counts,'expected_rows':len(rows)})\n(out/'atomic-manifest.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary,indent=2),encoding='utf-8'); print(summary)\n'@ | python -I Which one of the following, if true, most weakens the consumer advocate\'s argument?\\r\\nCHOICES {\'A\': \\"Providing information on minor drug-related interactions would detract from a patient\'s attention to serious interactions.\\", \'B\': \'Many drugs have fewer documented drug-related interactions than does aspirin.\', \'C\': \'Providing information about all drug-related interactions would result in only negligible price increases for consumers.\', \'D\': \'Current research is such that many drug-related interactions have not yet been identified.\', \'E\': \\"Pharmacists usually draw patients\' attention to printed warnings that are provided with drugs.\\"}\\r\\nSTEPS [(\'Conclusion (Therefore)\', \'The government should require drug companies to notify consumers of all known drug-related interactions.\'), (\'Evidence\', \'Relatively minor drug-related interactions can still be harmful to patients (such as the fact that fruit juice can render aspirin ineffective, leading to people either not getting the pain relief they expected or to people taking more aspirin than necessary).\'), (\'Evaluate\', \'This is a recommendation, so ask what could go wrong with the plan. It strengthens the case for the plan.\')]\\r\\nPT158-S4-Q1 D The government\'s tax collection agency has not followed through on its plan, announced a year ago, to crack down on violations of corporate income tax law. Audits are the primary tool for detecting such violations, and over the past year, not a single audit of corporate income tax returns has been completed. Which one of the following, if true, most weakens the argument?\\r\\nCHOICES {\'A\': \'The plan to crack down on violations of corporate income tax law is part of a broad campaign against corporate misconduct.\', \'B\': \'The number of personal income tax returns audited over the past year is greater than in previous years.\', \'C\': \'Most audits of corporate income tax returns do not reveal any significant violations.\', \'D\': \'It generally takes longer than one year to complete an audit of a corporate income tax return.\', \'E\': \'Over the last five years, fewer audits of corporate income tax returns have been completed than in the preceding five years.\'}\\r\\nSTEPS [(\'Conclusion\', \\"The government\'s tax collection agency has not followed through on its plan to crack down on violations of corporate income tax law.\\"), (\'Evidence\', \'Audits are the primary tool for detecting corporate income tax violations, and over the past year since they announced the plan, not a single audit of corporate income tax returns has been completed.\'), (\'Evaluate\', \'The author\\\\\'s case: audits are the primary way to detect violations, no corporate audit has been completed in a year, so the agency hasn\\\\\'t followed through on its crackdown.\\\\n\\\\nGiven that not a single corporate audit was completed in the past year, how could we still argue the agency is following through? Look at the word \\"completed.\\" The author assumes a real crackdown would produce finished audits within a year. What if audits take longer than that? Then the agency could have started hundreds of audits the day after the announcement, and the count of completed audits would still be zero.\\\\n\\\\nAnother route: the agency may be cracking down by other means. Audits are the \\"primary\\" tool, not the only one.\'), (\'Goal\', \'Look for an answer that gives an innocent reason for zero completed audits, most likely that audits take more than a year to finish. Or an answer showing the agency is cracking down some other way.\')]\\r\\nNOTES [(\'A\', \\"A says the crackdown is part of a broad campaign against corporate misconduct. That describes the plan\'s scope, not whether anything is being done. A broad campaign can still be doing nothing, and zero completed audits is just as unexplained.\\"), (\'B\', \'B is about personal income tax audits; the crackdown is about corporate income tax law. More personal audits shows the agency is busy, but not with this plan. If anything, it makes the lack of corporate audits look like a choice.\'), (\'C\', \\"C says most corporate audits don\'t turn up significant violations. That might make audits seem less worth doing, but it neither explains why none were completed nor shows any crackdown effort. The evidence of zero completed audits stands.\\"), (\'D\', \'The argument assumes a real crackdown would show completed audits within a year. D says it \\"generally takes longer than one year to complete an audit.\\" Now zero completed audits is exactly what we\\\\\'d expect even if the agency began auditing the day it announced the plan. The evidence no longer points to inaction.\\\\n\\\\nThe move to repeat: when evidence counts results over a period, ask whether results could even arrive in that time.\'), (\'E\', \\"E compares the last five years with the five before: fewer audits completed. That is a longer trend of doing less, which fits the idea that the agency isn\'t serious. It doesn\'t explain the past year\'s zero, so it can\'t weaken.\\")]\\r\\nPT158-S4-Q16 D Journalist: When judges do not maintain strict control over their courtrooms, lawyers often try to influence jury verdicts by using inflammatory language and by badgering witnesses. These obstructive behaviors hinder the jury\'s effort to reach a correct verdict. Whenever lawyers engage in such behavior, therefore, it is reasonable to doubt whether the verdict is correct. Which one of the following, if true, most weakens the journalist\'s argument?\\r\\nCHOICES {\'A\': \\"Court proceedings overseen by judges who are very strict in controlling lawyers\' behavior are known to result sometimes in incorrect verdicts.\\", \'B\': \'Lawyers tend to be less concerned than are judges about whether the outcomes of jury trials are just or not.\', \'C\': \'People who are influenced by inflammatory language are very unlikely to admit at some later time that they were influenced by such language.\', \'D\': \'Obstructive courtroom behavior by a lawyer is seldom effective in cases where jurors are also presented with legitimate evidence.\', \'E\': \'The selection of jurors is based in part on an assessment of the likelihood that they are free of bias.\'}\\r\\nSTEPS [(\'Conclusion\', \\"Whenever lawyers engage in obstructive behaviors (such as inflammatory language or badgering witnesses), it\'s reasonable to doubt whether the eventual verdict is correct.\\"), (\'Evidence\', \\"These obstructive behaviors hinder the jury\'s effort to reach a correct verdict.\\"), (\'Evaluate\', \'The argument slides between two ideas.\\\\n\\\\nIdea one: obstructive behavior hinders the jury\\\\\'s effort to reach a correct verdict.\\\\nIdea two: obstructive behavior makes it reasonable to doubt that a correct verdict was reached.\\\\n\\\\nA strong headwind might hinder my effort to reach the top of Mt. Whitney. But if I\\\\\'m standing at the summit, the headwind gives you no reason to doubt I got there.\\\\n\\\\nSame here. Obstructive behavior can make the jury\\\\\'s job harder without making a wrong verdict likely. Given that these behaviors hinder the jury, how could we argue the verdict still deserves trust? Show that juries usually get past the obstruction anyway. And notice \\"Whenever\\": the conclusion covers every trial with such behavior, so anything showing the behavior rarely changes outcomes hurts it.\'), (\'Goal\', \'Look for an answer saying these behaviors rarely change what the jury decides, so even when lawyers use them, the verdict is probably still correct.\')]\\r\\nNOTES [(\'A\', \\"A says strict judges sometimes preside over incorrect verdicts. The conclusion is about trials with obstructive behavior, and A is about trials without it. Wrong verdicts happening elsewhere doesn\'t show that badgering witnesses is harmless.\\"), (\'B\', \'B says lawyers care less than judges about whether outcomes are just. That is about motives. The question is whether the tactics actually distort verdicts, and lawyers who care little about justice are, if anything, more likely to try.\'), (\'C\', \'C says people swayed by inflammatory language rarely admit it later. That makes the influence harder to detect, which gives us more reason to doubt verdicts, not less. It pushes toward the conclusion.\'), (\'D\', \'The conclusion needs obstructive behavior to actually threaten the verdict. That happens before trial. A juror who starts out unbiased can still be swayed by badgering during it, so E doesn\'t show the behavior is harmless.\\")]\\r\\nPT158-S4-Q22 E Historian: Because medieval epistemology (theory of knowledge) is a complex subject, intellectual historians have, until recently, failed to produce a definition that would help to determine what should and what should not be included in it. Clearly, the solution is to define medieval epistemology simply as \\"the epistemological beliefs of the medieval epistemologists.\\" That way, if we want to know whether medieval epistemology includes some epistemological claim, we just ask whether any medieval epistemologists believed it. If any did, it is part of medieval epistemology; if any medieval epistemologists believed the opposite, then that opposite claim is part of medieval epistemology. Which one of the following, if true, would most weaken the historian\'s argument?\\r\\nCHOICES {\'A\': \'Medieval epistemologists held some of the same epistemological beliefs as did ancient epistemologists.\', \'B\': \'The epistemological beliefs of medieval epistemologists depended upon their beliefs about nonepistemological matters.\', \'C\': \'The writings of most medieval epistemologists include passages that are clearly not about epistemology.\', \'D\': \'Some medieval epistemologists had epistemological beliefs that contradicted the epistemological beliefs of other medieval epistemologists.\', \'E\': \'There is much debate as to which medieval thinkers, if any, were epistemologists.\'}\\r\\nSTEPS [(\'Conclusion\', \'The fix for the definition problem is to define medieval epistemology as \\"the epistemological beliefs of the medieval epistemologists.\\" The historian claims this definition will finally tell us what does and does not belong in medieval epistemology.\'), (\'Evidence\', \'Historians have failed to produce a definition that settles what belongs in medieval epistemology.\\\\n\\\\nWith the new definition, the test for any epistemological claim is simple: did any medieval epistemologist believe it? If one did, the claim is in. If one believed the opposite, the opposite claim is in too.\'), (\'Evaluate\', \'The test has one input: a list of the medieval epistemologists. Everything depends on having that list. How would we know who belongs on it? Presumably they are the thinkers who did medieval epistemology. But that is the very thing we are trying to define.\\\\n\\\\nSo given that the definition sounds tidy, how could we argue it still fails to settle what is included? Show that we cannot agree on who the medieval epistemologists were. Then the test has nothing to run on.\\\\n\\\\nNotice what is NOT a problem for the historian: disagreement. The historian already says that if one epistemologist believed X and another believed the opposite, both claims count. Contradictions among the epistemologists were built into the plan.\'), (\'Goal\', \\"Look for an answer that makes it hard to identify who the medieval epistemologists were. If we can\'t pick them out, the definition can\'t tell us what is included.\\\\n\\\\nWatch out for answers about epistemologists contradicting each other. The historian built that into the plan, so it doesn\'t weaken it.\\")]Traceback (most recent call last):\\r\\n\\r\\nNOTES [(\'A\', \\"Overlap with ancient thinkers doesn\'t stop the test from running. Ask the historian\'s question about a shared belief: did any medieval epistemologist believe it? Yes, so it is in. Where else the belief came from has no effect on whether the definition can sort claims.\\"), (\'B\', \'This is about why they held their epistemological beliefs, not which beliefs they held. The definition only asks whether a medieval epistemologist believed a claim. A belief shaped by \\"nonepistemological matters\\" is still one of their epistemological beliefs, and the test still sorts it.\'), (\'C\', \'The definition only collects their \\"epistemological beliefs,\\" and the historian only asks about \\"some epistemological claim.\\" Passages that are \\"clearly not about epistemology\\" are simply left out. Mixed writings make the reading longer, but they don\\\\\'t stop the test from deciding what is included.\'), (\'D\', \\"This is the trap the stimulus warned us about. The historian says that if any medieval epistemologists believed the opposite of a claim, that opposite claim also counts. Contradictions were planned for, so this answer describes something the definition already handles. It doesn\'t make the definition any harder to apply.\\"), (\'E\', \'The definition sorts claims by asking whether \\"any medieval epistemologists\\" believed them. That test needs an agreed list of who the epistemologists were. If there is \\"much debate\\" about which thinkers, \\"if any,\\" qualify, the test can\\\\\'t run, and the definition fails to settle what is included, which was the whole problem it promised to solve. The move to repeat: when a definition leans on a group, attack our ability to identify that group.\')]\\r\\nPT158-S4-Q24 D Consumer advocate: Some agricultural crops are now being genetically engineered to produce important pharmaceuticals. However, this development raises the possibility that the drugs will end up in the general food supply, since if pollen from a drug-producing crop drifts into a nearby field in which an ordinary, non-drug-producing crop of the same species is being grown, the pollen could fertilize that crop and turn it into a drug-producing crop as well. Which one of the following, if true, would most weaken the consumer advocate\'s argument?\\r\\nCHOICES {\'A\': \'As far as scientists know, none of the pharmaceuticals produced by genetically engineered crops would present any danger to public health if they were present in the general food supply.\', \'B\': \'If pollen from a genetically engineered crop is prevented from drifting into fields in which ordinary crops of the same species are being grown, then there is no risk of the pollen fertilizing the latter crops.\', \'C\': \'The genetically engineered crops that produce pharmaceuticals are not among the crop species that comprise the largest portions of the general food supply.\', \'D\': \'In crops genetically engineered to produce pharmaceuticals, the drugs are not present in any part of the plant used for food in ordinary crops of the same species.\', \'E\': \'If pollen from a drug-producing crop turned an ordinary crop of the same species into a drug-producing crop, it would be possible for scientists to identify the latter crop.\'}\\r\\nSTEPS   File \\"<stdin>\\", line 8, in <module>\\r\\n  File \\"C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\AppData\\\\Local\\\\Programs\\\\Python\\\\Python312\\\\Lib\\\\encodings\\\\cp1252.py\\", line 19, in encode\\r\\n    return codecs.charmap_encode(input,self.errors,encoding_table)[0]\\r\\n           ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^\\r\\nUnicodeEncodeError: \'charmap\' codec can\'t encode character \'\\\\u2192\' in position 470: character maps to <undefined>\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nimport pathlib,json,sys\nsys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding='utf-8')\np=pathlib.Path('.'); wanted={'PT157-S3-Q15','PT157-S3-Q25','PT157-S3-Q6','PT158-S2-Q21','PT158-S2-Q7','PT158-S3-Q24','PT158-S3-Q5','PT158-S4-Q24'}\nfor b in range(22,25):\n for f in json.loads((p/f'sql/e{b}-final.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8')):\n  if f['qid'] not in wanted: continue\n  pk=json.loads((p/f\"packets/{f['qid']}.json\").read_text(encoding='utf-8'))\n  print(f['qid'],pk['key_db'],pk['stimulus_db'],pk['stem_db'],pk['choices_db'])\n  print('STEPS',[(s['label'],s['body']) for s in f['steps']]);print('NOTES',[(n['letter'],n['body']) for n in f['notes']])\n'@ | python -I -","workdir":"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\claude\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\699dc587-39bd-4a08-a0c2-306235d0ae46\\scratchpad\\step3","max_output_tokens":12000}));
